Title: Evangelium Regni. A Joyfull Message of the Kingdom, published by the holy Spirit of the love of Jesus Christ, and sent forth unto all nations of people, which love the truth in Jesus Christ
Creator: Hendrik Niclaes (H. N.), founder of the Family of Love (translator from the Low German not named: "Translated out of Base-Almayn")
Publisher: London, no printer named ("Imprinted at London"), 1652 reprint of the Elizabethan English translation
Publication Date: 1652
Rights: Public Domain (published 1652; public domain by date)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_early-english-books-1641-1700_evangelium-regni-_niclas-hendrik_1652, file bim_early-english-books-1641-1700_evangelium-regni-_niclas-hendrik_1652_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for family-of-love-and-spiritualists-franck-niclaes), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: none on the item (no possible-copyright-status and no licence field; collections pub_early-english-books-*, bim_microfilm; an EEBO microfilm scan contributed by Internet Archive, not access-restricted).
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long-s to s normalisation (5740 occurrences). Rights basis restated: a 1652 London print, dated by the host catalogue and by the garbled imprint line in the OCR ("Impriated at Zonden. 26. 5 2."), is public domain by date; no modern editorial text is in the file.
Content note: The whole print: title page, THE PREFACE (file line 76 onward) and the forty-two chapters of the Evangelium Regni, headed "The N. Chapter" in roman numerals as printed (the OCR catches eleven of these headings cleanly, e.g. "The XI. Chapter" at line 3436 and "The XLII Chapter" at line 11946; the others are garbled). Niclaes's own central work, styled as a gospel, in the Elizabethan English translation reprinted in 1652 (no 1570s print of the English Evangelium Regni was found on archive.org). SECOND WITNESS for exact wording: EEBO microfilm OCR, roman type, sampled at five points (10/30/50/70/90 percent) and found readable with scattered broken words and misread letters (about 0.83 of tokens read as words); check quotations against the page images.
